### Task 6: The client-side duration pre-flight

PRD §5 wants a file longer than 15 minutes skipped **before a byte moves**. The only place that can happen is the browser, because the browser is the only party holding the file. So this task reads the duration out of the container header — a few dozen bytes, not the audio — and hands the answer to Task 4's `preflight()`.

Three things about this task are easy to get wrong, so they are stated before the code.

**It is not a security control.** The client lies, and nothing here stops it lying. A member who wants to upload a 90-minute mix can call the API with `client_duration_ms: 300000` and this module never runs. The pre-flight is a bandwidth optimisation and a UX affordance: it saves the honest user a 40-minute upload that would be deleted anyway, and it puts "6:12" next to the filename. The actual enforcement is two things that do not involve the client at all — the **size gate** in Task 4 (`maxBytesFor(container)`, re-run server-side in Task 7 before anything is signed) and **M3 decoding the file**, which sets `files.duration_ms`, deletes the object and marks the row `rejected_duration`. Global Constraint 15 already says the client's number is advisory forever; this task is where that number is produced, and it is produced knowing it will never be believed.

**Never `decodeAudioData`.** Global Constraint 16. `decodeAudioData` needs the whole file in an `ArrayBuffer` and then expands it to Float32 PCM — around 212 MB of RAM for a 10-minute stereo track. Multiply by any concurrency and the tab dies. The number we want lives in the first few dozen bytes of the file.

**Never hand the parser a truncated prefix.** This is the trap. `blob.slice(0, 65536)` looks like the cheap thing to do and it is *silently wrong*, not an error — see the byte budget below. `music-metadata`'s blob tokenizer already does the slicing, lazily and with random access; give it the whole `File`.

#### What actually gets read, per container

`music-metadata` v11 parses a `Blob` through `strtok3`'s `BlobTokenizer`, whose `peekBuffer` is exactly `blob.slice(pos, pos + len).arrayBuffer()` and whose `supportsRandomAccess()` returns `true`. Critically, `tokenizer.ignore(n)` on a blob is **pointer arithmetic only — zero bytes read** (`AbstractTokenizer.ignore` just advances `position` when `fileInfo.size` is known). That is what makes every case below cheap.

| Container | Where the duration is | What the parser touches |
|---|---|---|
| FLAC | STREAMINFO `totalSamples / sampleRate` — exact | First ~50 bytes. Verified: a 42-byte synthetic FLAC yields 372.000 s. Later metadata blocks (VORBIS_COMMENT, PICTURE) are `ignore`d with `skipCovers`. |
| WAV | `data` chunk size / `byteRate` — exact | RIFF header + `fmt ` + the `data` chunk **header**, then `ignore(chunkSize)`. Verified: **79 bytes read for an 8 MiB WAV**. |
| AIFF | `COMM` chunk `numSampleFrames / sampleRate` — exact | FORM + COMM, under 1 KB. `SSND` is `ignore`d. |
| MP4 / M4A | `moov` → `mvhd`/`mdhd` `duration / timescale` — exact | Walks 8/16-byte top-level atom headers from offset 0; `mdat` is `ignore`d, costing **nothing**. Then reads `moov` wherever it lives, typically 5–50 KB. |
| MP3 *with* Xing/Info/VBRI | frame count × 1152 / sampleRate — exact | ID3v2 tag (APIC frames `ignore`d under `skipCovers`) + the first audio frame. Usually under 32 KB. |
| MP3, CBR, no Xing | `mpegSize / frameSize × 1152 / sampleRate`, from the file size — near-exact | Same as above; `MpegParser` sets `codecProfile: 'CBR'` and stops. Verified: 208,500 bytes of 128 kbps frames → 13.06 s. |
| MP3, VBR, no Xing | **nowhere** | See below. |
| Ogg / Opus | last page's `granulePosition` — needs a tail read that `music-metadata` only performs with `duration: true`, which scans every page | We pass `duration: false`, so **Ogg and Opus report no duration**. Accepted: they are vanishingly rare in a DJ pool and the size gate covers them. |

**The moov-at-the-end case is the one that breaks naive implementations.** iTunes, ffmpeg without `-movflags +faststart`, and most phone recorders write `mdat` first and `moov` last, so the duration is in the *final* kilobytes of a 60 MB file. An implementation that reads "the first N bytes and parses those" gets `ftyp`, then `mdat`, then end-of-buffer, and reports no duration at all — every M4A silently becomes "length unknown". The fix is not a bigger N and it is not a tail slice either (`moov` is not always last). The fix is: **pass the whole `Blob` and let the tokenizer seek.** `ignore()` on a blob costs zero bytes, so walking past a 60 MB `mdat` to reach the `moov` behind it is free — two or three 16-byte reads and then the `moov` itself.

The same rule, stated as its failure mode and pinned by a test: parsing `wav.slice(0, 65536)` of the 8 MiB WAV above returns **0.371 s instead of 47.554 s**. Not an exception — a plausible, wrong number, with the truth buried in `metadata.quality.warnings`. A truncated prefix is worse than no answer.

#### A VBR MP3 with no Xing header cannot be measured from the header at all

There is no bitrate to multiply by, because the bitrate changes every frame, and there is no frame count, because the Xing/Info tag that would carry one is absent. `MpegParser` is explicit about it: with `options.duration !== true` it stops at the first frame and leaves `format.duration` **undefined**. Getting a real answer means walking every frame header to the end of the file — reading the whole file, which is the thing we are here to avoid.

The fallback is an estimate: `byteSize × 8 / format.bitrate`, where `format.bitrate` is one frame's bitrate. **It is a guess and it is wrong in both directions.** Measured on a synthetic alternating-128/192 kbps file: true duration 26.12 s, `format.bitrate` 192,000, estimate 21.73 s — 17% low. A file whose encoder ramps up from a quiet intro estimates high instead.

So the decision, and this is the judgement call this task makes:

> **An estimate never rejects a file, and never leaves the browser.** Only a `source: 'header'` duration is passed to `preflight()` and only a `source: 'header'` duration is sent as `ingest_begin`'s `p_client_duration_ms`. An estimate is shown in the UI, marked as an estimate, and thrown away.

The reasoning: a false rejection is unrecoverable from the user's side — the file is simply refused, with no way to appeal — while a false acceptance costs one upload that M3 deletes for free. And rejecting on a number that is 17% wrong would reject legitimate 14-minute tracks. `ingest_jobs.client_duration_ms` is documented in Task 2 as "what the browser pre-flight read out of the file header"; an extrapolation is not that, and writing one there would make the column mean two different things.

The hole this leaves, stated plainly: a headerless-VBR MP3 that is over 15 minutes long but under `maxBytesFor('mp3')` = 39.6 MB gets uploaded. At 128 kbps average that is anything up to ~41 minutes. It is caught by M3's decode, after the bytes have moved. Closing it in the browser would cost a full-file frame scan of every MP3 in a 200-file batch. Not worth it; M3 already downloads and decodes every file.

#### Why a Web Worker

200 files × a header parse each. Each parse is a chain of `await blob.slice(...).arrayBuffer()` calls, and every resolution runs parser code on whatever thread owns it. On the main thread that is 200 bursts of synchronous work interleaved with the dropzone's own rendering — the batch list stops scrolling and the progress bars stutter exactly while the user is watching them appear. The parse is also the only CPU work the upload path has (Constraint 11: the Worker never proxies bytes, and Task 8's transfer is XHR, which is I/O).

Design points:

- **One worker, not a pool.** The bottleneck is disk reads, not CPU, and each additional worker instantiates its own ~200 KB `music-metadata` module. Requests are serialised through a promise chain in `readDurationMs`.
- **Posting a `File` to a worker does not copy it.** `Blob`/`File` are cloneable by reference in the structured-clone algorithm — the bytes stay on disk and the worker's slices read them there. A 500 MB WAV costs nothing to hand over.
- **The timeout terminates the worker.** There is no portable way to cancel an in-flight `parseBlob` — no `AbortSignal` plumbing through the tokenizer. If a pathological file makes the parser scan, the only reliable stop is `worker.terminate()`; the next call constructs a fresh one. That is why `disposePreflightWorker()` is exported and why the timeout path calls it.
- **`new Worker(new URL('./preflight.worker.ts', import.meta.url), { type: 'module' })` must be written literally.** Vite detects that exact syntactic pattern to emit the worker bundle. Any indirection — `const W = globalThis.Worker`, a variable URL, a wrapper function — and the worker is silently not bundled, which fails at runtime in the production build only.
- **`music-metadata` resolves to the browser build inside a worker.** Its `exports` map has a `node` condition (`lib/index.js`, which pulls `node:stream`) and a `default` condition (`lib/core.js`, which does not). Vite resolves worker code with the browser/worker conditions, so `core.js` is what ships, and `parseBlob` is exported from it.

#### What can only be verified in a real browser

Vitest is node with no jsdom (Constraint 9). `Worker` is genuinely `undefined` there, `Blob` and `File` are node's, and disks behave differently from a browser's file-picker handles. Everything below is Step 8, by hand, with real files:

1. That Vite actually emits the worker chunk, in `astro dev` **and** in `astro build` — the two use different code paths and only the build one ships.
2. `moov`-at-the-end: an M4A from iTunes or from `ffmpeg` **without** `-movflags +faststart`. The synthetic fixtures cannot produce this.
3. An MP3 with 5 MB of embedded artwork in its ID3v2 tag — confirms `skipCovers` really skips rather than reads.
4. A 200-file drop: the UI stays interactive, memory does not climb (proving `File` went by reference), and total pre-flight time is seconds, not minutes.
5. That `PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000` is generous enough on an external drive or a network volume. If real files time out, raise it — do not lower the safety of the fallback.
6. Safari and Firefox module-worker support; if `new Worker(..., { type: 'module' })` throws, the inline fallback must run (it is exercised in node, but not the *throw* that triggers it in a browser).
7. The emitted worker chunk's size, once Task 8 imports it.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/lib/preflight.ts`
- Create: `src/lib/preflight.worker.ts`
- Test: `src/lib/preflight.test.ts`
- Modify: nothing. `music-metadata` was installed in Task 1 Step 8.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes, from `src/lib/upload-policy.ts` (Task 4): `preflight(filename: string, byteSize: number, clientDurationMs: number | null): PreflightVerdict`, `preflightMessage(v: PreflightVerdict): string`, `type PreflightVerdict`, `MAX_DURATION_MS`.
- Consumes, from Task 3: `ingest_begin(..., p_client_duration_ms int, ...)` — nullable, and this task passes `null` far more often than the naive reading of "the client measured it" suggests.
- Produces, from `src/lib/preflight.worker.ts`: `type HeaderFacts = { durationSec: number | null; bitrate: number | null; byteSize: number; container: string | null }`, `type PreflightRequest = { id: number; blob: Blob }`, `type PreflightResponse`, `parseAudioHeader(blob: Blob): Promise<HeaderFacts>`.
- Produces, from `src/lib/preflight.ts`: `PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `type DurationSource = 'header' | 'estimate' | 'unknown'`, `type DurationRead = { durationMs: number | null; source: DurationSource; note?: string }`, `UNKNOWN_DURATION`, `type FilePreflight = { verdict: PreflightVerdict; clientDurationMs: number | null; displayDurationMs: number | null; estimated: boolean; message: string }`, `classifyDuration(facts: HeaderFacts): DurationRead`, `preflightFile(file: { name: string; size: number }, read: DurationRead): FilePreflight`, `formatDuration(ms: number | null): string`, `readDurationMs(blob: Blob, timeoutMs?: number): Promise<DurationRead>`, `disposePreflightWorker(): void`.

Two deviations from the File Structure table, both deliberate:

- **`readDurationMs` resolves to a `DurationRead`, not a `number | null`.** A bare number cannot carry "this is an estimate", and that distinction is the entire point of the task. Task 8 must not treat the resolved value as a number.
- **It never rejects.** Every failure — unreadable bytes, worker crash, timeout, no `Worker` global — resolves to `{ durationMs: null, source: 'unknown' }`. A pre-flight that throws would abort a 200-file batch over one corrupt file.

Task 8 consumes it as: `const read = await readDurationMs(file)` → `const p = preflightFile(file, read)` → if `p.verdict.ok` is false, show `p.message` and skip; otherwise send `p.clientDurationMs` (which may be `null`) to `POST /api/upload/presign` and label the row with `formatDuration(p.displayDurationMs)`, suffixed `~` when `p.estimated`.

```bash
npm ls music-metadata
node -e "import('music-metadata').then(m => console.log(Object.keys(m).filter(k => k.startsWith('parse')).join(' ')))"
```

Expected: `music-metadata@11.x` in the tree, and `parseBlob parseBuffer parseFile parseFromTokenizer parseStream parseWebStream`. If `npm ls` reports it missing, Task 1 Step 8 was skipped — run `npm i aws4fetch music-metadata` before continuing.

The browser build is the `default` export condition (`lib/core.js`); the `node` condition (`lib/index.js`) is what the command above loads and is the only one that touches `node:stream`. Nothing in this task imports `parseFile` or `parseStream`, which is what keeps the worker bundle free of node built-ins.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing test**

Every fixture is bytes authored in the test file: no audio checked into the repo, no ffmpeg on the machine running CI. A 42-byte FLAC and a 44-byte RIFF header are enough to exercise the real parser, and hand-built MPEG frame headers reproduce both the CBR and the headerless-VBR cases exactly.

```ts
// src/lib/preflight.test.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest'
import {
  classifyDuration, preflightFile, formatDuration, readDurationMs, UNKNOWN_DURATION,
  type DurationRead,
} from './preflight'
import { parseAudioHeader } from './preflight.worker'
import { MAX_DURATION_MS } from './upload-policy'

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- fixtures
// Every fixture is bytes we author here. No .mp3 checked into the repo, and
// no ffmpeg on the test machine.

/** 42 bytes: 'fLaC' + a last-block STREAMINFO. Duration is totalSamples/rate. */
function synthFlac(totalSamples: number, sampleRate = 44100): Blob {
  const b = new Uint8Array(4 + 4 + 34)
  b.set([0x66, 0x4c, 0x61, 0x43], 0)          // 'fLaC'
  b[4] = 0x80                                  // last-metadata-block | type 0
  b[7] = 34                                    // block length
  const v = new DataView(b.buffer)
  v.setUint16(8, 4096); v.setUint16(10, 4096)  // min/max block size
  // 20 bits sampleRate | 3 bits (channels-1) | 5 bits (bps-1) | 36 bits samples
  const packed = (BigInt(sampleRate) << 44n) | (1n << 41n) | (15n << 36n) | BigInt(totalSamples)
  v.setBigUint64(18, packed)
  return new Blob([b], { type: 'audio/flac' })
}

/** A real 44-byte RIFF header followed by `dataBytes` of silence. */
function synthWav(dataBytes: number, sampleRate = 44100): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>[] {
  const channels = 2, bitsPerSample = 16
  const blockAlign = channels * (bitsPerSample / 8)
  const h = new Uint8Array(44)
  const v = new DataView(h.buffer)
  const ascii = (s: string, off: number) => {
    for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) h[off + i] = s.charCodeAt(i)
  }
  ascii('RIFF', 0); v.setUint32(4, 36 + dataBytes, true); ascii('WAVE', 8)
  ascii('fmt ', 12); v.setUint32(16, 16, true); v.setUint16(20, 1, true)
  v.setUint16(22, channels, true); v.setUint32(24, sampleRate, true)
  v.setUint32(28, sampleRate * blockAlign, true); v.setUint16(32, blockAlign, true)
  v.setUint16(34, bitsPerSample, true)
  ascii('data', 36); v.setUint32(40, dataBytes, true)
  return [h, new Uint8Array(dataBytes)]
}

/** MPEG-1 Layer III, 44.1 kHz, stereo, no CRC. frameSize = 144*bitrate/44100. */
const MP3_FRAME: Record<number, { byte2: number; size: number }> = {
  128: { byte2: 0x90, size: 417 },
  192: { byte2: 0xb0, size: 626 },
}

function mp3Frame(kbps: number): Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> {
  const f = MP3_FRAME[kbps]
  const b = new Uint8Array(f.size)
  b[0] = 0xff; b[1] = 0xfb; b[2] = f.byte2; b[3] = 0x00
  return b
}

/** One bitrate = CBR. Alternating bitrates = VBR, and no Xing header either way. */
function synthMp3(bitrates: number[], repeat: number): Blob {
  const parts: Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>[] = []
  for (let i = 0; i < repeat; i++) for (const k of bitrates) parts.push(mp3Frame(k))
  return new Blob(parts, { type: 'audio/mpeg' })
}

/** Counts every byte the parser actually pulls out of the blob. */
class CountingBlob extends Blob {
  bytesRead = 0
  slice(start?: number, end?: number, type?: string): Blob {
    this.bytesRead += (end ?? this.size) - (start ?? 0)
    return super.slice(start, end, type)
  }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- pure logic

describe('classifyDuration', () => {
  it('trusts a container-header duration', () => {
    expect(classifyDuration({ durationSec: 372, bitrate: 320_000, byteSize: 14_880_000, container: 'MPEG' }))
      .toEqual({ durationMs: 372_000, source: 'header' })
  })
  it('falls back to bitrate x size when the header carried no duration', () => {
    const read = classifyDuration({ durationSec: null, bitrate: 192_000, byteSize: 521_500, container: 'MPEG' })
    expect(read.source).toBe('estimate')
    expect(read.durationMs).toBe(Math.round((521_500 * 8 * 1000) / 192_000))
  })
  it('is unknown when there is neither', () => {
    expect(classifyDuration({ durationSec: null, bitrate: null, byteSize: 1000, container: null }).source)
      .toBe('unknown')
  })
  it('treats a zero or non-finite duration as absent', () => {
    expect(classifyDuration({ durationSec: 0, bitrate: null, byteSize: 10, container: null }).source).toBe('unknown')
    expect(classifyDuration({ durationSec: Number.NaN, bitrate: null, byteSize: 10, container: null }).source).toBe('unknown')
  })
})

describe('preflightFile', () => {
  const header = (ms: number): DurationRead => ({ durationMs: ms, source: 'header' })
  const estimate = (ms: number): DurationRead => ({ durationMs: ms, source: 'estimate' })

  it('sends a header duration to the server and accepts', () => {
    const p = preflightFile({ name: 'track.mp3', size: 14_000_000 }, header(372_000))
    expect(p.verdict.ok).toBe(true)
    expect(p.clientDurationMs).toBe(372_000)
    expect(p.estimated).toBe(false)
  })

  it('rejects a header duration over 15 minutes', () => {
    const p = preflightFile({ name: 'set.flac', size: 300_000_000 }, header(MAX_DURATION_MS + 1))
    expect(p.verdict).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'too_long' })
    expect(p.message).toContain('15 minutes')
  })

  // THE decision of this task: an estimate never rejects, and never reaches
  // ingest_jobs.client_duration_ms. The size gate and M3's decode are the
  // enforcement. A first-frame bitrate is wrong in BOTH directions.
  it('never rejects on an estimate, and never sends one', () => {
    const p = preflightFile({ name: 'vbr.mp3', size: 20_000_000 }, estimate(1_200_000))
    expect(p.verdict.ok).toBe(true)
    expect(p.clientDurationMs).toBeNull()
    expect(p.displayDurationMs).toBe(1_200_000)
    expect(p.estimated).toBe(true)
    expect(p.message).toContain('estimate')
  })

  it('still applies the size gate to an estimate', () => {
    const p = preflightFile({ name: 'dj-set.mp3', size: 288_000_000 }, estimate(300_000))
    expect(p.verdict).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'too_large' })
  })

  it('accepts a file whose duration could not be read at all', () => {
    const p = preflightFile({ name: 'weird.ogg', size: 9_000_000 }, UNKNOWN_DURATION)
    expect(p.verdict.ok).toBe(true)
    expect(p.clientDurationMs).toBeNull()
    expect(p.displayDurationMs).toBeNull()
  })

  it('rejects an unsupported container whatever the duration says', () => {
    const p = preflightFile({ name: 'mix.exe', size: 10 }, header(1000))
    expect(p.verdict).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'unsupported_container' })
  })
})

describe('formatDuration', () => {
  it('is mm:ss', () => {
    expect(formatDuration(372_000)).toBe('6:12')
    expect(formatDuration(9_000)).toBe('0:09')
    expect(formatDuration(null)).toBe('--:--')
  })
})

// ------------------------------------------------- real music-metadata parse

describe('parseAudioHeader', () => {
  it('reads FLAC duration out of STREAMINFO in 42 bytes', async () => {
    const facts = await parseAudioHeader(synthFlac(44_100 * 372))
    expect(facts.container).toBe('FLAC')
    expect(facts.durationSec).toBeCloseTo(372, 6)
    expect(classifyDuration(facts)).toEqual({ durationMs: 372_000, source: 'header' })
  })

  it('reads an 8 MiB WAV without reading the audio', async () => {
    const blob = new CountingBlob(synthWav(8 * 1024 * 1024), { type: 'audio/wav' })
    const facts = await parseAudioHeader(blob)
    expect(facts.durationSec).toBeCloseTo(47.554, 3)
    // Constraint 16: never pull the payload into memory. 79 bytes, measured.
    expect(blob.bytesRead).toBeLessThan(4096)
  })

  it('is silently WRONG on a truncated prefix — never slice before parsing', async () => {
    const whole = new Blob(synthWav(8 * 1024 * 1024), { type: 'audio/wav' })
    const facts = await parseAudioHeader(whole.slice(0, 65536))
    expect(facts.durationSec).toBeCloseTo(0.371, 3)   // not 47.554, and no error
  })

  it('gets a CBR MP3 duration from the frame header plus the file size', async () => {
    const facts = await parseAudioHeader(synthMp3([128], 500))
    expect(facts.container).toBe('MPEG')
    expect(facts.durationSec).toBeCloseTo(13.06, 2)
    expect(classifyDuration(facts).source).toBe('header')
  })

  it('gets NO duration from a VBR MP3 with no Xing header', async () => {
    const facts = await parseAudioHeader(synthMp3([128, 192], 500))
    expect(facts.durationSec).toBeNull()
    expect(facts.bitrate).toBeGreaterThan(0)
    const read = classifyDuration(facts)
    expect(read.source).toBe('estimate')
    // True length is 1000 frames x 1152 / 44100 = 26.12 s. The estimate is
    // ~21.7 s — 17% low — because one frame's bitrate is not the average.
    expect(read.durationMs! / 1000).toBeLessThan(26)
  })

  it('rejects bytes it cannot identify, rather than inventing a duration', async () => {
    await expect(parseAudioHeader(new Blob([new Uint8Array(64)]))).rejects.toThrow()
  })
})

describe('readDurationMs', () => {
  // Node has Blob but no Worker, so this exercises the inline fallback path
  // that a browser without module workers would take. The worker path itself
  // is browser-only and is verified by hand in Step 8.
  it('falls back to an inline parse when there is no Worker global', async () => {
    expect(typeof Worker).toBe('undefined')
    expect(await readDurationMs(synthFlac(44_100 * 372)))
      .toEqual({ durationMs: 372_000, source: 'header' })
  })

  it('never throws — an unreadable file is an unknown duration', async () => {
    const read = await readDurationMs(new Blob([new Uint8Array(64)]))
    expect(read.source).toBe('unknown')
    expect(read.durationMs).toBeNull()
  })
})
```

Note `Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>[]` rather than `Uint8Array[]` in the fixture signatures. TypeScript 5.7+ made typed arrays generic over their buffer, and a bare `Uint8Array` annotation widens to `Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>`, which is not assignable to `BlobPart` (a `SharedArrayBuffer` cannot back a `Blob`). Without the type argument `npx tsc --noEmit` fails in Step 7 with three copies of TS2345 while the tests pass — an inconsistency that wastes a debugging cycle.

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement the worker**

Its only job is bytes → facts. It does **not** import `./preflight`, and it does not classify: keeping the classification on the main thread means there is no import cycle, no second copy of the decision logic in the worker chunk, and no nested-worker reference for Vite to resolve.

```ts
// src/lib/preflight.worker.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

import { parseBlob } from 'music-metadata'

export type HeaderFacts = {
  durationSec: number | null
  bitrate: number | null
  byteSize: number
  container: string | null
}

export type PreflightRequest = { id: number; blob: Blob }

export type PreflightResponse =
  | { id: number; ok: true; facts: HeaderFacts }
  | { id: number; ok: false; error: string }

const positive = (v: unknown): number | null =>
  typeof v === 'number' && Number.isFinite(v) && v > 0 ? v : null

/**
 * Read the container header, and nothing else.
 *
 * `duration: false` is the whole point of this module. With `true`,
 * music-metadata will walk every MPEG frame or every Ogg page to work out a
 * duration the header did not give it — i.e. read the entire file. With
 * `false` it reports only what the header states, which is exactly the
 * number we are allowed to have before bytes move.
 *
 * `skipCovers` makes embedded artwork an `ignore()` (zero bytes off a Blob)
 * instead of a read; `skipPostHeaders` skips the ID3v1/APE trailer probe,
 * saving one tail slice per file.
 *
 * The WHOLE blob goes in. Never a prefix: an MP4 written without faststart
 * keeps its `moov` atom behind a 60 MB `mdat`, and a truncated slice reports
 * a plausible wrong duration rather than an error.
 */
export async function parseAudioHeader(blob: Blob): Promise<HeaderFacts> {
  const md = await parseBlob(blob, {
    duration: false,
    skipCovers: true,
    skipPostHeaders: true,
  })
  return {
    durationSec: positive(md.format.duration),
    bitrate: positive(md.format.bitrate),
    byteSize: blob.size,
    // Advisory only. Task 4's extension decides the size cap and the key
    // suffix, and M3's ffprobe is authoritative for the real container.
    // Rejecting on a mismatch here would refuse valid files whose extension
    // is merely wrong, which is not a decision M2 gets to make.
    container: md.format.container ?? null,
  }
}

const scope = globalThis as unknown as {
  postMessage(message: PreflightResponse): void
  addEventListener(type: string, listener: (event: { data: PreflightRequest }) => void): void
  WorkerGlobalScope?: new () => unknown
}

// True only inside a worker: undefined in node (vitest imports this module
// directly), and on the main thread `globalThis` is a Window, not a
// WorkerGlobalScope. Importing this file must have no side effects anywhere
// else.
const inWorkerScope =
  typeof scope.WorkerGlobalScope !== 'undefined' && globalThis instanceof scope.WorkerGlobalScope

if (inWorkerScope) {
  scope.addEventListener('message', (event) => {
    const { id, blob } = event.data
    parseAudioHeader(blob).then(
      (facts) => scope.postMessage({ id, ok: true, facts }),
      (err: unknown) =>
        scope.postMessage({
          id, ok: false,
          error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),
        }),
    )
  })
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement the decision logic and the worker client**

```ts
// src/lib/preflight.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

import { preflight, preflightMessage, type PreflightVerdict } from './upload-policy'
import type { HeaderFacts, PreflightRequest, PreflightResponse } from './preflight.worker'

/**
 * Per-file budget for a header parse. Generous: a header parse is tens of
 * bytes, but they come off a disk that may be asleep, external or a network
 * volume. Exceeding it is treated as "unknown duration", never as a
 * rejection.
 */
export const PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000

/**
 * - `header`   the container states the duration. Exact or near-exact.
 * - `estimate` the container did NOT state it; extrapolated from one frame's
 *              bitrate. Wrong in both directions. Display only.
 * - `unknown`  no usable number at all.
 */
export type DurationSource = 'header' | 'estimate' | 'unknown'

export type DurationRead = {
  durationMs: number | null
  source: DurationSource
  note?: string
}

export const UNKNOWN_DURATION: DurationRead = {
  durationMs: null,
  source: 'unknown',
  note: 'not read',
}

/** Pure. Everything the browser-only path resolves to goes through here. */
export function classifyDuration(facts: HeaderFacts): DurationRead {
  if (facts.durationSec !== null && Number.isFinite(facts.durationSec) && facts.durationSec > 0) {
    return { durationMs: Math.round(facts.durationSec * 1000), source: 'header' }
  }
  if (facts.bitrate !== null && facts.bitrate > 0 && facts.byteSize > 0) {
    // A VBR MP3 with no Xing header. `bitrate` is ONE frame's bitrate, not
    // the average, so this is a guess: measured 17% low on a synthetic
    // 128/192 file. It exists to put a number next to the filename and it
    // never leaves the browser.
    return {
      durationMs: Math.round((facts.byteSize * 8 * 1000) / facts.bitrate),
      source: 'estimate',
      note: 'no duration in the container header; extrapolated from one frame',
    }
  }
  return { durationMs: null, source: 'unknown', note: 'no duration in the container header' }
}

export type FilePreflight = {
  verdict: PreflightVerdict
  /** Send to ingest_begin as p_client_duration_ms. Null unless it came from
   *  the container header — Task 2 documents that column as exactly that. */
  clientDurationMs: number | null
  /** Show in the UI. May be an estimate; see `estimated`. */
  displayDurationMs: number | null
  estimated: boolean
  message: string
}

/**
 * Pure. The decision, in one place.
 *
 * Only a header duration can reject a file. An estimate is never a
 * rejection: a false reject is unrecoverable for the user, a false accept
 * costs one upload that M3 deletes for free. The size gate in `preflight()`
 * still runs on every file whatever the duration says, and it is the only
 * pre-bytes check that does not depend on the client telling the truth.
 */
export function preflightFile(
  file: { name: string; size: number }, read: DurationRead,
): FilePreflight {
  const trusted = read.source === 'header' ? read.durationMs : null
  const verdict = preflight(file.name, file.size, trusted)
  return {
    verdict,
    clientDurationMs: trusted,
    displayDurationMs: read.durationMs,
    estimated: read.source !== 'header',
    message:
      verdict.ok && read.source === 'estimate'
        ? 'ready — length is an estimate until the file is analysed'
        : preflightMessage(verdict),
  }
}

export function formatDuration(ms: number | null): string {
  if (ms === null || !Number.isFinite(ms) || ms < 0) return '--:--'
  const total = Math.round(ms / 1000)
  return `${Math.floor(total / 60)}:${String(total % 60).padStart(2, '0')}`
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------- the worker

let worker: Worker | null = null
let workerUnavailable = false
let nextId = 1
let chain: Promise<unknown> = Promise.resolve()

function getWorker(): Worker | null {
  if (workerUnavailable || typeof Worker === 'undefined') return null
  if (!worker) {
    try {
      // This exact syntax is load-bearing: Vite detects `new Worker(new
      // URL('...', import.meta.url), { type: 'module' })` statically to emit
      // the worker bundle. Any indirection and the worker is silently not
      // bundled, which fails only in the production build.
      worker = new Worker(new URL('./preflight.worker.ts', import.meta.url), { type: 'module' })
    } catch {
      // No module-worker support. Parse inline instead: janky, but correct.
      workerUnavailable = true
      return null
    }
  }
  return worker
}

export function disposePreflightWorker(): void {
  worker?.terminate()
  worker = null
}

function parseInWorker(w: Worker, blob: Blob, timeoutMs: number): Promise<HeaderFacts> {
  const id = nextId++
  return new Promise<HeaderFacts>((resolve, reject) => {
    const onMessage = (event: MessageEvent) => {
      const data = event.data as PreflightResponse
      if (data.id !== id) return
      cleanup()
      if (data.ok) resolve(data.facts)
      else reject(new Error(data.error))
    }
    const onError = () => {
      cleanup()
      disposePreflightWorker()
      reject(new Error('preflight worker crashed'))
    }
    const timer = setTimeout(() => {
      cleanup()
      // There is no portable way to cancel an in-flight parseBlob — no
      // AbortSignal reaches the tokenizer. Killing the worker is the only
      // reliable stop; getWorker() builds a fresh one for the next file.
      disposePreflightWorker()
      reject(new Error('preflight timed out'))
    }, timeoutMs)
    function cleanup() {
      clearTimeout(timer)
      w.removeEventListener('message', onMessage)
      w.removeEventListener('error', onError)
    }
    w.addEventListener('message', onMessage)
    w.addEventListener('error', onError)
    // Structured clone passes a Blob/File BY REFERENCE — the bytes stay on
    // disk, so handing over a 500 MB WAV costs nothing.
    const request: PreflightRequest = { id, blob }
    w.postMessage(request)
  })
}

/**
 * Read a file's duration without reading the file.
 *
 * Never rejects: a corrupt file in a 200-file batch must not abort the
 * batch, so every failure resolves to `unknown`. Serialised through one
 * worker — the bottleneck is disk reads, not CPU, and a pool would mean one
 * copy of music-metadata per worker.
 */
export function readDurationMs(
  blob: Blob, timeoutMs: number = PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS,
): Promise<DurationRead> {
  const run = async (): Promise<DurationRead> => {
    try {
      const w = getWorker()
      const facts = w
        ? await parseInWorker(w, blob, timeoutMs)
        : await (await import('./preflight.worker')).parseAudioHeader(blob)
      return classifyDuration(facts)
    } catch (err) {
      return {
        durationMs: null,
        source: 'unknown',
        note: err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'header parse failed',
      }
    }
  }
  const result = chain.then(run, run)
  chain = result.then(() => undefined, () => undefined)
  return result
}
```

```js

const input = Object.assign(document.createElement('input'), { type: 'file', multiple: true })
input.click()                      // pick real audio, including an M4A and a big FLAC
```

### Task 7: The bulk upload client — queue, journal, transfer, and the UI

Everything the browser does between "the user dropped a folder" and "every row is `received`". Four modules and one island, in dependency order: a pure concurrency pump, an IndexedDB identity journal, an XHR transfer layer with a retry policy, and the Solid component that wires them together.

**Interface assumptions, reconciled against Task 5's actual routes.**
Task 5 owns `src/pages/api/upload/{batch,presign,parts,complete,abort,resume}.ts`. The wire format below is Task 5's real JSON — snake_case keys, exactly as `Response.json(...)` builds them there — not a paraphrase. `uploader.ts`'s internal types stay camelCase for the rest of this task's TypeScript; `httpApi`, in Step 11, is the one place that translates between the two, so the wire format's snake_case never leaks past it.

| Route | Method | Request | 2xx response |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/api/upload/batch` | POST | `{ label? }` | 201 `{ batch_id }` |
| `/api/upload/presign` | POST | `{ batch_id, file_id, filename, byte_size, client_duration_ms }` | `{ file_id, state, resumable:true, multipart:false, url, content_type, expires_at }` or `{ file_id, state, resumable:true, multipart:true, upload_id, part_size, part_count, content_type }` or `{ file_id, state, resumable:false, done }` |
| `/api/upload/parts` | POST | `{ file_id, from, to }` — an **inclusive, contiguous 1-based range**, capped at `MAX_PART_URLS_PER_CALL = 100` per call, never an arbitrary list of part numbers | `{ file_id, part_size, byte_size, expires_at, parts:[{ part_number, start, end, url }] }` |
| `/api/upload/complete` | POST | `{ file_id, parts? }` — `parts` is `[{ part_number, etag }]` or omitted/`null` to mean "ask R2 what landed" | `{ file_id, state:"received", byte_size }` (sometimes with `already:true` on a replay) |
| `/api/upload/abort` | POST | `{ file_id, reason? }` | `{ file_id, state:"failed" }` |
| `/api/upload/resume` | GET | `?file_id=` | `{ file_id, state, multipart, upload_id, part_size, part_count, byte_size, uploaded_parts:[{ part_number, etag, size }], expired? }` |

Five things follow from that table and Task 3, and each one is a decision this task makes:

1. **`presign` calls `ingest_begin` *and then* `ingest_mark_uploading`.** Presigning is the act that makes bytes movable, so it is the correct place for the `pending → uploading` transition, and it saves the client a round trip per file across 200 files. `parts` calls `ingest_mark_uploading(file_id, null)` too — Task 3's `coalesce(p_upload_id, j.upload_id)` exists precisely so that re-presigning parts 50–99 refreshes `updated_at` without blanking the `UploadId` the sweeper needs.
2. **`presign` returns `resumable:false, done:true` when `ingest_begin` replays a row already in `received`, and `resumable:false, done:false` for `failed`/`abandoned`.** The `done:true` case is what makes a blind re-drop of a finished batch free; `done:false` means the row is dead and a fresh `file_id` is required (see the retry table below).
3. **`etag` on the wire is the unquoted hex string.** R2 returns `ETag: "9b2c…"` with quotes; the client strips them (`normaliseEtag`). Task 5's `buildCompleteMultipartUpload` in `s3-xml.ts` re-adds them before talking to R2 — the client never sees a quoted ETag and never has to re-add the quotes itself.
4. **`content_type` comes from the presign response and is echoed verbatim on the PUT.** The client never derives it with `contentTypeFor()`, because a client that disagrees with what the server signed produces a `SignatureDoesNotMatch` that looks like an expired URL.
5. **Presigned URLs must be signed for at least 3600 s.** With `FILE_CONCURRENCY × PART_CONCURRENCY = 9` streams sharing one uplink, a 16 MiB part on a bad venue link can take minutes. A short TTL turns every large file into a 403 storm. Task 5's `PRESIGN_TTL_SECONDS = 3600` already satisfies this.

**Task 6's `readDurationMs(blob: Blob): Promise<DurationRead>`** is what this task consumes — not a bare `number | null`. `DurationRead = { durationMs: number | null; source: 'header' | 'estimate' | 'unknown'; note?: string }`, and it never rejects. A bare number cannot express "this is an estimate", and Task 6's own measurements show a VBR MP3 without a Xing header estimates ~17% low — the UI has to be able to mark that with a `~`, and the server must never be sent a number the client cannot stand behind. Concretely: `const read = await readDurationMs(file)` → `const p = preflightFile(file, read)` (also from Task 6, which wraps Task 4's `preflight()`/`preflightMessage()` so this task does not call them directly) → if `p.verdict.ok` is false, show `p.message` and skip; otherwise send `p.clientDurationMs` (which may be `null`) as `client_duration_ms` to `POST /api/upload/presign`, and label the row with Task 6's `formatDuration(p.displayDurationMs)`, suffixed `~` when `p.estimated` — this task imports `formatDuration` from `src/lib/preflight.ts` rather than writing its own.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/lib/upload-queue.ts`
- Create: `src/lib/upload-queue.test.ts`
- Create: `src/lib/upload-journal.ts`
- Create: `src/lib/upload-journal.test.ts`
- Create: `src/lib/uploader.ts`
- Create: `src/lib/uploader.test.ts`
- Create: `src/components/UploadDropzone.tsx`
- Create: `src/pages/upload.astro`
- Modify: `src/pages/index.astro` — a link to `/upload`, or the page is unreachable

`upload-journal.test.ts` and `uploader.test.ts` are the File Structure table's test files for those two modules. Both export pure functions — `journalKey`, `classifyStatus`, `backoffDelayMs`, `normaliseEtag`, `contiguousRanges` — and Global Constraint 9 is explicit that pure logic gets a unit test. The IndexedDB fallback path is testable in node for free, because node has no `indexedDB` and the fallback is what runs.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `planUpload`, `partRange`, `type UploadPlan` from `src/lib/upload-policy.ts` (Task 4); `readDurationMs`, `preflightFile`, `formatDuration`, `type DurationRead` from `src/lib/preflight.ts` (Task 6) — never `preflight`/`preflightMessage` directly, since `preflightFile` already wraps them; the six routes above (Task 5); `Astro.locals.member` (M1).
- Produces:
  - `upload-queue.ts` — `FILE_CONCURRENCY`, `PART_CONCURRENCY`, `PREFLIGHT_CONCURRENCY`, `type PumpTask<T>`, `type PumpResult<T>`, `type PumpOptions<T>`, `pump()`.
  - `upload-journal.ts` — `JOURNAL_DB`, `JOURNAL_STORE`, `JOURNAL_TTL_MS`, `type JournalEntry`, `type FileIdentity`, `journalKey()`, `rememberFile()`, `lookupFile()`, `forgetFile()`, `pruneJournal()`.
  - `uploader.ts` — `MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `BACKOFF_BASE_MS`, `BACKOFF_CAP_MS`, `PART_PRESIGN_CHUNK`, `type Disposition`, `classifyStatus()`, `backoffDelayMs()`, `normaliseEtag()`, `contiguousRanges()`, `TransferError`, `SessionExpiredError`, `ApiError`, `UploadFailure`, `isAbortError()`, `xhrPut()`, `type PresignResponse`, `type PresignedPart`, `type UploadApi`, `httpApi`, `type UploadItem`, `uploadFile()`.
  - `UploadDropzone.tsx` — default export, props `{ userId: string }`.
- Consumed by: Task 8 (manual verification) only. Nothing in M3 imports any of this.

---

#### The decisions this task makes

**Bounded concurrency: 3 files at a time, 3 parts within a file, 4 header reads.**
200 files must not open 200 sockets. The numbers are small on purpose and the reason is not socket count — R2's S3 endpoint speaks HTTP/2, so the browser's six-connections-per-origin limit does not bind. Three reasons do:

- **A single uplink.** Past about four concurrent streams a home or venue uplink gains nothing; all that changes is that every file finishes at the end instead of some finishing early. With a bounded pump, file 1 is `received` and durable while file 200 has not started. With 200 in parallel, a tab close at 90% loses 200 partial uploads instead of 20.
- **Presign TTL.** URLs are signed when the transfer starts, not when the batch is planned. Bounded concurrency and just-in-time presigning are the same mechanism: no URL sits unused for an hour.
- **Progress that means something.** `xhr.upload.onprogress` across 200 simultaneous transfers is 200 bars all crawling. Across 3 it is three bars moving and 197 rows saying "queued", which is the truth.

Worst case in flight is `3 × 3 = 9` PUTs, asserted in the test. Header reads are pooled separately at 4 because Task 6 spawns a Web Worker per read; 200 simultaneous Workers is a real way to hang a tab.

**`XMLHttpRequest`, not `fetch`.** Global Constraint 14. `fetch` has no upload progress event — `ReadableStream` request bodies (`duplex: 'half'`) would allow counting bytes as they are pulled, but they are not supported for cross-origin requests in Safari and they disable HTTP/1.1 fallback. `xhr.upload.onprogress` is the only portable way to drive a per-file bar. The tradeoff is real and worth naming: XHR gives up streaming response bodies, `AbortSignal` composition (we bridge it manually to `xhr.abort()`), and modern error typing — a cross-origin network failure surfaces as `status === 0` with no cause, indistinguishable from a CORS misconfiguration. That last one is why `classifyStatus(0)` is `retry` and why the manual checklist below insists on watching the network panel once against real R2.

**Idempotency: the journal maps a re-picked file back to its existing `file_id`.**
`files.id` is client-generated and is the idempotency key (Task 2's column comment). A retried upload must reuse it or `ingest_begin` mints a second row and a second R2 object. The journal key is

```
`${userId}|${size}|${lastModified}|${name}`
```

Field order matters. The Decisions section says `name|size|lastModified`; putting the free-form field **last**, behind two integers and a UUID, makes the key injective — with `name` in front, a file literally called `a|100|5` could collide with a different file called `a`. Same information, no ambiguity. `userId` is in the key because two members can share a browser profile: without it, member B re-picks a file member A uploaded, gets A's `file_id`, and `ingest_begin` raises `42501 forbidden` for reasons no one can debug from the UI.

This is a heuristic, not a content identity. It is deliberately not a hash — the PRD forbids hashing a 200-file batch up front, and Web Crypto has no streaming digest (Decisions, `content_sha256`). A false match needs three fields to agree: same name, same byte count, same mtime. In practice that means the same file, which is the case we want to match. When it is genuinely a different file:

- If the matched row is already `received`, `presign` returns `resumable:false, done:true` and the row shows "already uploaded". The new content is silently not uploaded. This is also the correct behaviour for a real re-pick, and it is recoverable — the user renames the file and drops it again.
- If the matched row is mid-flight, resuming would splice new bytes into an old multipart at old offsets. `uploadFile` blocks that: it compares the server's `partSize` against the plan for the current `File`, and drops the journal entry and mints a fresh `file_id` when they disagree. Since `size` is part of the key, a differing size cannot reach that code path at all — the key is simply a miss.

Accepted cost: same name, same size, same mtime, different bytes, first upload finished. Ten users, a DJ pool, and M3 hashes every object anyway, so a genuinely wrong object is caught one layer down. Paying for it up front means SHA-256 over ~55 GB in a browser tab.

**Retry policy.** Bounded at 5 attempts per PUT, full-jitter exponential backoff (`random() * min(20 s, 500 ms × 2^attempt)`). Full jitter rather than equal jitter because a 200-file batch produces synchronised retries and full jitter is what actually decorrelates them.

| Outcome | Disposition | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 200 / 201 / 204 | `ok` | — |
| status 0 | `retry` | Network drop, DNS, or a CORS failure. Indistinguishable from XHR; retrying costs one round trip and the manual checklist covers the CORS case. |
| 403 | `represign` | `SignatureDoesNotMatch` / expired URL. Fetch a fresh URL for that exact part, then back off and retry. |
| 404 | `restart` | `NoSuchUpload` — the 24 h sweeper or the R2 lifecycle rule already aborted this multipart. Nothing to resume. |
| 408 / 429 / any 5xx | `retry` | Transient. `Retry-After` is not honoured: R2 does not send it on PUT and the jittered backoff is already in range. |
| 400 / 401 / 413 / 415 / 422 | `fatal` | Malformed, signed out, too large, wrong type, rejected. Retrying is guaranteed to fail identically. |

**Which failures tear the server row down, and which do not.** This one is load-bearing and comes straight out of Task 3. `POST /api/upload/abort` calls `ingest_fail`, which moves the row to `failed`. `ingest_mark_uploading` accepts only `pending` and `uploading` as source states. **A row in `failed` can never be resumed.** So:

- `fatal` and `restart` → call `/api/upload/abort`. The multipart is torn down immediately instead of billing until the sweeper runs, and the row is correctly terminal. The Retry button on such a row calls `forgetFile()` and mints a **new** `file_id`.
- Exhausted `retry`/`represign` → **do not** call abort. Leave the row in `uploading` and the multipart alive in R2, so Retry resumes it from the parts already there. If the user never comes back, the 24 h sweeper and the 1-day R2 lifecycle rule clean up. This is exactly what Decisions' "tab close mid-batch" paragraph is for.
- `POST /api/upload/complete` answering `409 size_mismatch` or `409 object_missing` already means Task 5 called `ingest_fail` server-side before responding — the row is `failed` before the client even sees the error. `readJson()` turns that into an `ApiError`, which is not a `TransferError` and so classifies as `fatal` in `uploadFile`'s catch block: `discard` is `true`, the (now redundant, and harmless) `abort` call is idempotent, and Retry mints a fresh `file_id`. `GET /api/upload/resume` answering `expired:true` is handled explicitly in `transferMultipart` — it throws a `restart`-disposition `TransferError` rather than trying to resume an `upload_id` R2 already discarded, landing in the same fresh-`file_id` path.

*(Follow-up for whoever revisits Task 3, not litigated here: adding `'failed'` to `ingest_mark_uploading`'s source array would let an aborted row be resumed too. Task 7 works with the machine as specified.)*

**Resume, concretely.** User drops 200 files, walks away, closes the tab at file 140. Next morning, same or different device, `/upload`, re-drops the same folder:

1. `enqueue()` computes `journalKey(userId, file)` for each `File`. 140 hit the journal (Chrome persists IndexedDB across sessions; on a different device none hit, and the flow degrades to a fresh upload, which is correct).
2. Rows with a hit render "started before — will resume", and a banner offers **Resume** or **Start fresh**. Start fresh calls `forgetFile()` and mints new `file_id`s; the old rows are left to the sweeper.
3. Start → `POST /api/upload/batch` mints a new batch. Resumed files keep their original `batch_id`, because `ingest_begin` returns early on replay and ignores the new one. That is right: the file belongs to the drop that started it.
4. Per file, `POST /api/upload/presign` replays `ingest_begin`. Files 1–139 come back `resumable:false, done:true` — one small JSON each, zero bytes moved, row shows "already uploaded".
5. File 140 comes back `resumable:true, multipart:true` with the **stored** `upload_id`, `part_size` and `part_count`. Nothing was recomputed; `part_size` lives in Postgres for this reason (Global Constraint 12).
6. `GET /api/upload/resume?file_id=…` runs `ListParts` against R2 and returns the parts that survived, with their ETags and sizes. **We never persisted those ETags** — R2 is the source of truth, which is what makes step 1's cross-device miss the only thing that breaks on a new device.
7. The client seeds its progress baseline from `sum(completed.size)`, computes the missing part numbers, groups them into contiguous ranges of at most 100 (`contiguousRanges`, since `/api/upload/parts` accepts one `[from, to]` range per call, never an arbitrary list), presigns each range, and uploads only those parts.
8. `POST /api/upload/complete` sends the merged list — recovered ETags plus fresh ones, sorted by part number. The server completes the multipart, `HEAD`s the object, and calls `ingest_finalize` with the verified size.

**Solid, not React.** `createSignal` for scalars, `createStore` for the row list so a progress tick updates one `<progress>` and not 200 rows, `<For>` not `.map`, `class` not `className`, `onInput` not `onChange`, `props.userId` never destructured, `onMount` for anything touching `window` or `indexedDB` — the island is server-rendered for its initial HTML and the component body runs in the Worker. **`File` objects are held in a plain `Map` outside the store**, because `createStore` deep-proxies plain objects and a proxied `File` is no longer something `xhr.send()` will accept.

**What node cannot test.** `pump()`, `journalKey()`, `classifyStatus()`, `backoffDelayMs()`, `normaliseEtag()` and `contiguousRanges()` are pure and fully covered. `XMLHttpRequest`, `IndexedDB`, `DataTransfer`, drag events, `beforeunload` and the island itself are not, and there is no jsdom (Global Constraint 9). The checklist in Step 15 is the whole verification for those, and it must run against a real R2 bucket.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test for the pump**

Create `src/lib/upload-queue.test.ts`:

```ts
// src/lib/upload-queue.test.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest'
import {
  pump, FILE_CONCURRENCY, PART_CONCURRENCY, PREFLIGHT_CONCURRENCY,
  type PumpTask,
} from './upload-queue'

const tick = () => new Promise<void>((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 0))

function deferred<T>() {
  let resolve!: (value: T) => void
  const promise = new Promise<T>((res) => { resolve = res })
  return { promise, resolve }
}

describe('pump', () => {
  it('runs every task and returns results in input order', async () => {
    const delays = [30, 0, 15, 5]
    const tasks: PumpTask<number>[] = delays.map((ms, i) => async () => {
      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms))
      return i
    })
    const results = await pump(tasks, { concurrency: 2 })
    expect(results.map((r) => r.index)).toEqual([0, 1, 2, 3])
    expect(results.map((r) => (r.status === 'fulfilled' ? r.value : null))).toEqual([0, 1, 2, 3])
  })

  it('never exceeds the requested concurrency', async () => {
    let inFlight = 0
    let peak = 0
    const tasks: PumpTask<number>[] = Array.from({ length: 20 }, (_, i) => async () => {
      inFlight += 1
      peak = Math.max(peak, inFlight)
      await tick()
      inFlight -= 1
      return i
    })
    const results = await pump(tasks, { concurrency: 3 })
    expect(peak).toBe(3)
    expect(results).toHaveLength(20)
  })

  it('pulls from the iterator lazily rather than materialising every task', async () => {
    // This is the "200 files must not open 200 sockets" property, asserted.
    let produced = 0
    const gate = deferred<void>()
    function* generate(): Generator<PumpTask<number>> {
      for (let i = 0; i < 200; i += 1) {
        produced += 1
        yield async () => { await gate.promise; return i }
      }
    }
    const run = pump(generate(), { concurrency: 3 })
    await tick()
    expect(produced).toBe(3)
    gate.resolve()
    await run
    expect(produced).toBe(200)
  })

  it('a rejected task does not stop the batch', async () => {
    const tasks: PumpTask<string>[] = [
      async () => 'a',
      async () => { throw new Error('boom') },
      async () => 'c',
    ]
    const results = await pump(tasks, { concurrency: 2 })
    expect(results[0]).toEqual({ index: 0, status: 'fulfilled', value: 'a' })
    expect(results[1].status).toBe('rejected')
    expect(results[2]).toEqual({ index: 2, status: 'fulfilled', value: 'c' })
  })

  it('never rejects, even when every task throws', async () => {
    const tasks: PumpTask<never>[] = Array.from({ length: 5 }, () => async () => {
      throw new Error('nope')
    })
    const results = await pump(tasks, { concurrency: 2 })
    expect(results.every((r) => r.status === 'rejected')).toBe(true)
  })

  it('aborts running tasks and skips the ones that had not started', async () => {
    const controller = new AbortController()
    const started: number[] = []
    const abortSeen: number[] = []
    const gate = deferred<void>()
    const tasks: PumpTask<string>[] = Array.from({ length: 6 }, (_, i) => async (signal) => {
      started.push(i)
      signal.addEventListener('abort', () => abortSeen.push(i), { once: true })
      await gate.promise
      return `done-${i}`
    })

    const run = pump(tasks, { concurrency: 2, signal: controller.signal })
    await tick()
    expect(started).toEqual([0, 1])

    controller.abort(new Error('user cancelled'))
    gate.resolve()
    const results = await run

    expect([...abortSeen].sort()).toEqual([0, 1])
    expect(started).toEqual([0, 1])
    expect(results[0]).toEqual({ index: 0, status: 'fulfilled', value: 'done-0' })
    expect(results.slice(2).every((r) => r.status === 'skipped')).toBe(true)
  })

  it('skips everything when the signal is already aborted', async () => {
    const controller = new AbortController()
    controller.abort(new Error('too late'))
    let ran = 0
    const tasks: PumpTask<number>[] = Array.from({ length: 4 }, () => async () => {
      ran += 1
      return 1
    })
    const results = await pump(tasks, { concurrency: 2, signal: controller.signal })
    expect(ran).toBe(0)
    expect(results.every((r) => r.status === 'skipped')).toBe(true)
  })

  it('calls onSettled once per task, in completion order', async () => {
    const order: number[] = []
    const delays = [30, 5, 15]
    const tasks: PumpTask<number>[] = delays.map((ms, i) => async () => {
      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms))
      return i
    })
    await pump(tasks, { concurrency: 3, onSettled: (r) => order.push(r.index) })
    expect(order).toEqual([1, 2, 0])
  })

  it('returns an empty array for an empty input', async () => {
    expect(await pump([], { concurrency: 3 })).toEqual([])
  })

  it('tolerates a concurrency larger than the task count', async () => {
    const tasks: PumpTask<number>[] = [async () => 1, async () => 2]
    const results = await pump(tasks, { concurrency: 50 })
    expect(results.map((r) => (r.status === 'fulfilled' ? r.value : null))).toEqual([1, 2])
  })

  it('refuses a non-positive concurrency', async () => {
    await expect(pump([], { concurrency: 0 })).rejects.toThrow('positive integer')
    await expect(pump([], { concurrency: 1.5 })).rejects.toThrow('positive integer')
  })
})

describe('concurrency constants', () => {
  it('keeps the worst case in-flight PUT count at nine or fewer', () => {
    expect(FILE_CONCURRENCY).toBe(3)
    expect(PART_CONCURRENCY).toBe(3)
    expect(PREFLIGHT_CONCURRENCY).toBe(4)
    expect(FILE_CONCURRENCY * PART_CONCURRENCY).toBeLessThanOrEqual(9)
  })
})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the pump**

Create `src/lib/upload-queue.ts`:

```ts
// src/lib/upload-queue.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

/**
 * How many files transfer at once.
 *
 * Not a socket-count limit — R2's S3 endpoint speaks HTTP/2, so the browser's
 * six-connections-per-origin cap does not bind. Three because:
 *
 *  - A single uplink saturates around four streams. Past that, nothing gets
 *    faster; the only change is that every file finishes at the end instead
 *    of some finishing early. Bounded, file 1 is durable in R2 while file 200
 *    has not started, so a tab close at 90% loses 3 partial uploads, not 200.
 *  - Presigned URLs are minted when a transfer starts, not when the batch is
 *    planned, so no URL sits unused until it expires.
 *  - Three moving progress bars is legible. Two hundred crawling bars is not.
 */
export const FILE_CONCURRENCY = 3

/**
 * How many 16 MiB parts of ONE file transfer at once. Worst case in flight is
 * FILE_CONCURRENCY * PART_CONCURRENCY = 9 PUTs, which is why presigned URLs
 * must be signed for at least an hour: nine streams sharing a venue uplink
 * make a single part slow.
 */
export const PART_CONCURRENCY = 3

/**
 * How many duration pre-flights run at once. Separate from the transfer pool
 * because each one spawns a Web Worker (Task 6); 200 simultaneous Workers is
 * a reliable way to hang a tab.
 */
export const PREFLIGHT_CONCURRENCY = 4

/** A unit of work. Receives the pump's signal so it can cancel its own I/O. */
export type PumpTask<T> = (signal: AbortSignal) => Promise<T>

export type PumpResult<T> =
  | { index: number; status: 'fulfilled'; value: T }
  | { index: number; status: 'rejected'; reason: unknown }
  /** Aborted before this task ever started. It did no work. */
  | { index: number; status: 'skipped' }

export interface PumpOptions<T> {
  concurrency?: number
  signal?: AbortSignal
  /** Fires once per task, in COMPLETION order. Results are returned in input order. */
  onSettled?: (result: PumpResult<T>) => void
}

/**
 * Bounded-concurrency runner over an iterable of async tasks.
 *
 * Pure: no DOM, no network, no timers of its own. Two properties the upload
 * client depends on:
 *
 *  1. It NEVER rejects. One unreadable file must not kill a 200-file batch,
 *     so a throwing task becomes a `rejected` result and the pump continues.
 *  2. It pulls from the iterator lazily, at most `concurrency` tasks ahead.
 *     Passing a generator therefore means 200 files never produce 200 live
 *     closures, 200 Blob slices or 200 sockets.
 */
export async function pump<T>(
  tasks: Iterable<PumpTask<T>>,
  options: PumpOptions<T> = {},
): Promise<PumpResult<T>[]> {
  const concurrency = options.concurrency ?? FILE_CONCURRENCY
  if (!Number.isInteger(concurrency) || concurrency < 1) {
    throw new Error('pump: concurrency must be a positive integer')
  }

  const iterator = tasks[Symbol.iterator]()
  const results: PumpResult<T>[] = []

  // An inner controller so a task's own failure never aborts its siblings,
  // and so the outer signal is relayed exactly once.
  const controller = new AbortController()
  const outer = options.signal
  const relay = () => controller.abort(outer?.reason)
  if (outer) {
    if (outer.aborted) controller.abort(outer.reason)
    else outer.addEventListener('abort', relay, { once: true })
  }

  let issued = 0
  const settle = (result: PumpResult<T>) => {
    results[result.index] = result
    options.onSettled?.(result)
  }

  // iterator.next() is synchronous, and JS is single-threaded, so the workers
  // cannot hand out the same index twice.
  const worker = async (): Promise<void> => {
    for (;;) {
      const step = iterator.next()
      if (step.done === true) return
      const index = issued
      issued += 1
      if (controller.signal.aborted) {
        settle({ index, status: 'skipped' })
        continue
      }
      try {
        settle({ index, status: 'fulfilled', value: await step.value(controller.signal) })
      } catch (reason) {
        settle({ index, status: 'rejected', reason })
      }
    }
  }

  try {
    await Promise.all(Array.from({ length: concurrency }, () => worker()))
  } finally {
    outer?.removeEventListener('abort', relay)
  }
  return results
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the failing test for the journal**

Node has no `indexedDB`, so `openDb()` resolves `null` and every call falls through to the in-memory map. That is not a mock — it is the same fallback that runs in Safari private mode, and testing it here is the only automated coverage it gets.

Create `src/lib/upload-journal.test.ts`:

```ts
// src/lib/upload-journal.test.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

import { describe, it, expect, beforeEach } from 'vitest'
import {
  journalKey, rememberFile, lookupFile, forgetFile, pruneJournal,
  JOURNAL_TTL_MS,
} from './upload-journal'

const USER = '00000000-0000-0000-0000-0000000000e1'
const identity = { name: 'Artist - Title.flac', size: 40_000_000, lastModified: 1_700_000_000_000 }

describe('journalKey', () => {
  it('is userId|size|lastModified|name', () => {
    expect(journalKey(USER, identity))
      .toBe(`${USER}|40000000|1700000000000|Artist - Title.flac`)
  })

  it('changes when only lastModified changes', () => {
    expect(journalKey(USER, { ...identity, lastModified: identity.lastModified + 1 }))
      .not.toBe(journalKey(USER, identity))
  })

  it('changes when only the size changes', () => {
    expect(journalKey(USER, { ...identity, size: identity.size + 1 }))
      .not.toBe(journalKey(USER, identity))
  })

  it('is stable across two picks of the same file', () => {
    expect(journalKey(USER, { ...identity })).toBe(journalKey(USER, { ...identity }))
  })

  it('cannot be forged by a pipe in the filename', () => {
    // The free-form field is LAST, behind a uuid and two integers, so no
    // filename can impersonate another file's key. This is why the field
    // order is not the name|size|lastModified of the prose.
    const sneaky = journalKey(USER, { name: '1|2|real.flac', size: 9, lastModified: 9 })
    const real = journalKey(USER, { name: 'real.flac', size: 9, lastModified: 9 })
    expect(sneaky).not.toBe(real)
  })
})

describe('the in-memory fallback (node has no indexedDB)', () => {
  const key = journalKey(USER, identity)

  beforeEach(async () => {
    await forgetFile(key)
  })

  it('remembers and looks up a file id', async () => {
    await rememberFile({
      key, userId: USER, fileId: 'f1', batchId: 'b1',
      name: identity.name, size: identity.size, lastModified: identity.lastModified,
    })
    const hit = await lookupFile(key)
    expect(hit?.fileId).toBe('f1')
    expect(hit?.batchId).toBe('b1')
  })

  it('returns null for a key it has never seen', async () => {
    expect(await lookupFile('nope')).toBeNull()
  })

  it('forgets a key', async () => {
    await rememberFile({
      key, userId: USER, fileId: 'f1', batchId: 'b1',
      name: identity.name, size: identity.size, lastModified: identity.lastModified,
    })
    await forgetFile(key)
    expect(await lookupFile(key)).toBeNull()
  })

  it('prunes entries older than the TTL and keeps fresh ones', async () => {
    const now = 2_000_000_000_000
    await rememberFile({
      key: 'stale', userId: USER, fileId: 'old', batchId: 'b0',
      name: 'a.mp3', size: 1, lastModified: 1,
    }, now - JOURNAL_TTL_MS - 1)
    await rememberFile({
      key: 'fresh', userId: USER, fileId: 'new', batchId: 'b0',
      name: 'b.mp3', size: 1, lastModified: 1,
    }, now)
    await pruneJournal(now)
    expect(await lookupFile('stale')).toBeNull()
    expect((await lookupFile('fresh'))?.fileId).toBe('new')
  })
})
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement the journal**

Create `src/lib/upload-journal.ts`:

```ts
// src/lib/upload-journal.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

/**
 * The browser's half of "tab close mid-batch: the batch survives, the browser
 * does not have to".
 *
 * This stores ONE thing: name|size|lastModified -> file_id. Not part ETags —
 * ListParts recovers those from R2 on demand, which is what makes resume work
 * on a different device. Not upload_id or part_size either; those live in
 * Postgres so the sweeper can abort an orphaned multipart with no browser
 * involved.
 */

export const JOURNAL_DB = 'localchune-upload-journal'
export const JOURNAL_STORE = 'files'

/** Entries older than this are pruned on mount. A month is far longer than
 *  the 24 h sweeper window, so nothing useful is ever thrown away. */
export const JOURNAL_TTL_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000

export interface FileIdentity {
  name: string
  size: number
  lastModified: number
}

export interface JournalEntry extends FileIdentity {
  key: string
  userId: string
  fileId: string
  batchId: string
  updatedAt: number
}

/**
 * The identity heuristic. A HEURISTIC, not a content identity: it is three
 * fields off the File handle, and the PRD forbids hashing a 200-file batch up
 * front (Web Crypto has no streaming digest, so a hash means holding each
 * whole file in an ArrayBuffer).
 *
 * Field order is deliberate. `userId` first, because two members can share a
 * browser profile and handing member B member A's file_id makes ingest_begin
 * raise 42501 for reasons nobody can debug from the UI. The free-form `name`
 * LAST, behind a uuid and two integers, so the key is injective — with the
 * name in front, a file called `a|100|5` could collide with a file called `a`.
 *
 * A false match needs the same name, the same byte count AND the same mtime.
 * In practice that is the same file, which is the case this exists to catch.
 * When it is not: if the matched row already reached `received` the file is
 * reported "already uploaded" and the new bytes are silently not sent
 * (recoverable — rename and re-drop); if it is mid-flight, uploadFile()
 * compares the server's stored part_size against the plan for the current
 * File and starts fresh when they disagree. M3 hashes every object anyway.
 */
export function journalKey(userId: string, file: FileIdentity): string {
  return `${userId}|${file.size}|${file.lastModified}|${file.name}`
}

// Fallback store. This is what runs in node (no indexedDB at all) and in
// Safari private mode / a hardened profile where indexedDB.open() throws.
// Losing cross-session resume is acceptable; failing the whole batch is not.
const memory = new Map<string, JournalEntry>()

let dbPromise: Promise<IDBDatabase | null> | null = null

function openDb(): Promise<IDBDatabase | null> {
  if (dbPromise !== null) return dbPromise
  dbPromise = new Promise<IDBDatabase | null>((resolve) => {
    if (typeof indexedDB === 'undefined') { resolve(null); return }
    let request: IDBOpenDBRequest
    try {
      request = indexedDB.open(JOURNAL_DB, 1)
    } catch {
      resolve(null)
      return
    }
    request.onupgradeneeded = () => {
      const db = request.result
      if (!db.objectStoreNames.contains(JOURNAL_STORE)) {
        const store = db.createObjectStore(JOURNAL_STORE, { keyPath: 'key' })
        store.createIndex('updatedAt', 'updatedAt')
      }
    }
    request.onsuccess = () => resolve(request.result)
    request.onerror = () => resolve(null)
    request.onblocked = () => resolve(null)
  })
  return dbPromise
}

function withStore<T>(
  mode: IDBTransactionMode,
  run: (store: IDBObjectStore) => IDBRequest,
): Promise<T | null> {
  return openDb().then((db) => {
    if (db === null) return null
    return new Promise<T | null>((resolve) => {
      let request: IDBRequest
      try {
        request = run(db.transaction(JOURNAL_STORE, mode).objectStore(JOURNAL_STORE))
      } catch {
        resolve(null)
        return
      }
      request.onsuccess = () => resolve(request.result as T)
      request.onerror = () => resolve(null)
    })
  })
}

/**
 * Write the mapping BEFORE the first byte moves. A journal entry written after
 * a successful upload is useless — the case it has to survive is the tab
 * closing at 40%.
 */
export async function rememberFile(
  entry: Omit<JournalEntry, 'updatedAt'>,
  now: number = Date.now(),
): Promise<void> {
  const row: JournalEntry = { ...entry, updatedAt: now }
  memory.set(row.key, row)
  await withStore('readwrite', (store) => store.put(row))
}

export async function lookupFile(key: string): Promise<JournalEntry | null> {
  const stored = await withStore<JournalEntry | undefined>('readonly', (store) => store.get(key))
  return stored ?? memory.get(key) ?? null
}

export async function forgetFile(key: string): Promise<void> {
  memory.delete(key)
  await withStore('readwrite', (store) => store.delete(key))
}

export async function pruneJournal(now: number = Date.now()): Promise<void> {
  const cutoff = now - JOURNAL_TTL_MS
  for (const [key, row] of memory) {
    if (row.updatedAt < cutoff) memory.delete(key)
  }
  const db = await openDb()
  if (db === null) return
  await new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
    let cursor: IDBRequest<IDBCursorWithValue | null>
    try {
      cursor = db
        .transaction(JOURNAL_STORE, 'readwrite')
        .objectStore(JOURNAL_STORE)
        .index('updatedAt')
        .openCursor(IDBKeyRange.upperBound(cutoff))
    } catch {
      resolve()
      return
    }
    cursor.onsuccess = () => {
      const c = cursor.result
      if (c === null) { resolve(); return }
      c.delete()
      c.continue()
    }
    cursor.onerror = () => resolve()
  })
}
```

```ts
// src/lib/uploader.test.ts
// localchune — MIT licensed. See LICENSE.
// NOTE: the distributed combination is AGPL-3.0 because the analysis
// worker includes Essentia. LICENSE explains why.

import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest'
import {
  classifyStatus, backoffDelayMs, normaliseEtag, contiguousRanges,
  MAX_ATTEMPTS, BACKOFF_BASE_MS, BACKOFF_CAP_MS, PART_PRESIGN_CHUNK,
} from './uploader'

describe('classifyStatus', () => {
  it('treats 200, 201 and 204 as success', () => {
    for (const s of [200, 201, 204]) expect(classifyStatus(s)).toBe('ok')
  })

  it('retries a status of 0', () => {
    // XHR reports a network drop, a DNS failure and a CORS rejection
    // identically as status 0. Retrying costs one round trip; the CORS case
    // is caught by the manual checklist, not by guessing here.
    expect(classifyStatus(0)).toBe('retry')
  })

  it('re-presigns on 403', () => {
    expect(classifyStatus(403)).toBe('represign')
  })

  it('restarts on 404', () => {
    // NoSuchUpload: the 24 h sweeper or the R2 lifecycle rule already aborted
    // this multipart. There is nothing left to resume.
    expect(classifyStatus(404)).toBe('restart')
  })

  it('retries 408, 429 and every 5xx', () => {
    for (const s of [408, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504]) expect(classifyStatus(s)).toBe('retry')
  })

  it('fails permanently on 400, 401, 413, 415 and 422', () => {
    for (const s of [400, 401, 413, 415, 422]) expect(classifyStatus(s)).toBe('fatal')
  })
})

describe('backoffDelayMs', () => {
  it('is zero when the RNG returns zero', () => {
    expect(backoffDelayMs(0, () => 0)).toBe(0)
    expect(backoffDelayMs(4, () => 0)).toBe(0)
  })

  it('is full jitter bounded by base * 2^attempt', () => {
    // Full jitter, not equal jitter: a 200-file batch produces synchronised
    // retries, and full jitter is what actually decorrelates them.
    const almostOne = () => 0.999999
    expect(backoffDelayMs(0, almostOne)).toBeLessThan(BACKOFF_BASE_MS)
    expect(backoffDelayMs(1, almostOne)).toBeLessThan(BACKOFF_BASE_MS * 2)
    expect(backoffDelayMs(2, almostOne)).toBeLessThan(BACKOFF_BASE_MS * 4)
  })

  it('is capped', () => {
    expect(backoffDelayMs(30, () => 0.999999)).toBeLessThanOrEqual(BACKOFF_CAP_MS)
  })

  it('is always a non-negative integer', () => {
    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_ATTEMPTS; attempt += 1) {
      const d = backoffDelayMs(attempt)
      expect(Number.isInteger(d)).toBe(true)
      expect(d).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0)
    }
  })
})

describe('normaliseEtag', () => {
  it('strips the quotes R2 wraps the header in', () => {
    expect(normaliseEtag('"9b2cf5341234abcd"')).toBe('9b2cf5341234abcd')
  })

  it('strips a weak-validator prefix', () => {
    expect(normaliseEtag('W/"9b2cf5341234abcd"')).toBe('9b2cf5341234abcd')
  })

  it('is null when the header is missing', () => {
    // The CORS symptom: without ExposeHeaders: ["ETag"] in the bucket rule,
    // getResponseHeader('ETag') is null and multipart completion is
    // impossible. It presents as an inexplicable undefined, not as an error.
    expect(normaliseEtag(null)).toBeNull()
    expect(normaliseEtag('')).toBeNull()
  })
})

describe('contiguousRanges', () => {
  it('is one range for a gap-free list', () => {
    expect(contiguousRanges([1, 2, 3, 4], 100)).toEqual([[1, 4]])
  })

  it('splits at a gap', () => {
    // /api/upload/parts accepts exactly one inclusive [from, to] range per
    // call, never an arbitrary list — a gap from an out-of-order retry has
    // to become two range requests, not one that silently spans it.
    expect(contiguousRanges([4, 5, 9, 10, 11], 100)).toEqual([[4, 5], [9, 11]])
  })

  it('splits a run longer than maxLength', () => {
    expect(contiguousRanges([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], 2)).toEqual([[1, 2], [3, 4], [5, 5]])
  })

  it('returns nothing for an empty list', () => {
    expect(contiguousRanges([], 100)).toEqual([])
  })

  it('never emits a range longer than PART_PRESIGN_CHUNK in practice', () => {
    const all = Array.from({ length: 250 }, (_, i) => i + 1)
    for (const [from, to] of contiguousRanges(all, PART_PRESIGN_CHUNK)) {
      expect(to - from + 1).toBeLessThanOrEqual(PART_PRESIGN_CHUNK)
    }
  })
})
```
